Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured Three rules, because breaking\nany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n\n1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n   lands in another agent's tree.\n2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n   one that commits to it. There is no\nprivate extension mechanism, no plugin API that only Anthropic-shaped packages\nget, and no marketplace: a package is checked-in source in your own repository,\nregistered by one static import.\n\nReference implementations, in increasing size:\n\n| Package | What it shows |\n|---|---|\n| [`examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard`](../examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard) | the smallest honest package: one resource, one action, one policy — **written as a customer would write it** |\n| [`packages/delivery`](../packages/delivery) | a package that **depends on another package** through a declared capability |\n| [`packages/contracts`](../packages/contracts) | a package that **offers** a capability to others |\n\n## 1. First decide whether you need a package at all\n\nMost requests do not.\n\n| You need | Use |\n|---|---|\n| one new object with fields, CRUD, API, Admin and tests | the **module factory** — `npm run crm -- module create <manifest>.json --apply` (`docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md`) |\n| one lifecycle step on an existing record | a **record action** in `packages/actions/generated/index.js` (`docs/ACTIONS.md`) |\n| one versioned business rule | a **policy** on an existing registry (scoring, discount, activation…) |\n| a bounded domain: several related records, its own invariants, its own actions and evidence, installable and removable as a unit | a **domain package** |\n\nA package is justified when it owns **all** of these:\n\n- more than one related resource;\n- lifecycle or business invariants that must hold across them;\n- actions or workflows that enforce those invariants;\n- evidence records that explain what happened and why;\n- a meaningful \"install it / remove it\" boundary.\n\nA single custom object with a couple of fields is a module. Making it a package\nadds a registration file, a README, a schema block and a conformance suite in\nexchange for nothing.\n\n**Do not** create a package to hold shared helpers, to work around a kernel\nlimitation, or to \"organize\" code you already have. If you need a kernel change\nto make your package work, that is a missing runtime capability: raise it as\none rather than reaching into `packages/core/src`.\n\n### Then start from a scaffold, not from somebody else's domain\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- package scaffold field-service            # a plan; writes nothing\nnpm run crm -- package scaffold field-service --apply    # two files\n```\n\nYou get exactly two files — `src/index.js` and `README.md` — with an identity, a\ncontract version and five **empty** declarations. That is already a conforming\npackage: `crm package test packages/field-service` exits 0 on it before you have\ntyped anything, so every edit you make from here starts from a known-good\nbaseline instead of from a domain you then have to subtract.\n\nIt deliberately generates **no** record, action, policy, capability, provider,\nAdmin section, Solution Plan or MCP tool. A generated `status` field is a\ndecision about a business nobody described, and a decision you have to notice\nbefore you can delete it is worse than an empty list. It also does not compose\nthe package, run a migration, open a database or install anything — see §12.\n\nAn occupied directory is refused rather than overwritten, and a name the\nregistry would reject is refused **with a suggestion** rather than quietly\nrenamed. It checks the target directory, not the composed application: a name\nalready registered by another package is refused at startup by the registry, and\n`crm app inspect --json` is what shows you that once the package is composed.\n\nTwo things worth knowing before you automate it. A plan **reserves nothing** —\n`--apply` re-checks the target and answers `TARGET_CLAIMED` if something got\nthere first. And because a plan and an apply both exit 0, read **`modeReason`**\nrather than the exit code to learn whether anything was written; an explicit\n`--dry-run` beats `--apply`. An interrupted earlier run blocks nothing: its\nstaging directory is reported as `staleStaging` for you to remove, never\ndeleted automatically.\n\n`--into <dir>` puts the package somewhere other than `packages/`; `--json`\ngives an agent the plan, its file hashes and a `fingerprint`.\nExecPlan: `docs/plans/dx3-package-scaffold.md`.\n\n## 2. Pick a canonical identity\n\n```js\npackageContract: 1        // the contract you are written against\nname: 'partner-scorecard' // lowercase, digits and hyphens; unique in the app\nversion: 1                // your package's own version, a positive integer\nlabel: 'Partner scorecard'\n```\n\nThe name is a Map key in the registry and a key in `/api/schema`. It is also\nwhat a collision is reported against, so choose something a stranger reading a\nstack trace would recognise.\n\n## 3. Declare what you need from other packages\n\nA package may reach another package **only** through a declared capability:\n\n```js\nrequires: [{ package: 'contracts', capability: 'delivery-obligations', version: 1 }],\n```\n\nThe registry resolves this at startup. A missing package, a capability the\nnamed package does not offer, a version mismatch or a dependency cycle stops\nthe application with the missing edge named — never at runtime, inside a\ntransaction.\n\nTo open it (inside an action, where you have `domains`):\n\n```js\nconst obligations = domains.capability({\n  consumer: 'delivery',\n  capability: 'delivery-obligations',\n  version: 1,\n  context: { modules, actor, now },   // YOUR runtime handles\n});\n```\n\nBecause the capability is created with the caller's `modules`, everything it\nreads and writes happens **inside the caller's transaction** — a cross-package\ncommit is atomic without either package sharing a database handle. A package\nthat did not declare the requirement is refused even when the capability exists\n(`CAPABILITY_NOT_DECLARED`), and a declaration naming the wrong provider is\nrefused too (`CAPABILITY_PROVIDER_MISMATCH`). The registry hands out no\ndefinition and no mutable index, so there is no second route to the same\ninterface — see \"What the contract does and does not enforce\" below for the\nline this stops at.\n\nOffering one is the mirror image:\n\n```js\ncapabilities: [{\n  name: 'delivery-obligations',\n  version: 1,\n  description: 'Read pending delivery obligations and mark them handed over.',\n  create({ modules, actor, now }) { return { /* the bounded interface */ }; },\n}],\n```\n\nExpose the smallest interface that does the job. `packages/contracts` offers\nthree methods and no service, table or query handle — that is the standard.\n\n## 4. Create package-owned resources\n\nRecords are ordinary manifests, applied by the ordinary module factory:\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- module create packages/<name>/modules/<record>.module.json --apply","numLines":150,"startLine":1,"totalLines":545}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a2/fixture/.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package\n\n## Orient yourself first\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n```\n\nRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n\nIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n\nThe package contract itself is enforced by a command, not by a document: `npm run crm -- package validate <dir>` runs the same validator startup runs, and its problems are the authority on whether a package is well-formed.\n\n**Background, where they exist:** `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` and `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-018 and its addenda). The reference packages, where the project carries them, are `examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard` (smallest, customer-authored), `packages/delivery` (depends on another package) and `packages/contracts` (offers a capability). They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n\n## Decide whether it is a package at all\n\n1. One object with fields and CRUD → **module factory**, not a package. One lifecycle step on an existing record → **an action**. One versioned rule → **a policy**. Say so and do that instead; a package for a single object is overhead with no boundary.\n2. A package is justified only when it owns several related resources, invariants across them, the actions that enforce those invariants, its own evidence records, and a real install/remove boundary.\n3. Never create a package to hold shared helpers or to work around a kernel limitation.\n\n## Start from a scaffold\n\n1. `npm run crm -- package scaffold <name>` prints a plan and writes nothing; `--apply` writes exactly two files, `src/index.js` and `README.md`, with an identity and five **empty** declarations. That output already passes `package validate`, `package inspect` and `package test` — start from it rather than from a copy of somebody else's domain.\n2. It generates **no** record, action, policy, capability, provider, Admin section, Solution Plan or MCP tool, and that is deliberate. Do not treat the empty lists as something to be filled by pattern-matching another package: every entry you add is a decision about this business.\n3. It composes nothing, opens no database and runs no migration. An occupied directory is refused, never overwritten; an invalid name is refused **with a suggestion**, never silently renamed — if you get `PACKAGE_NAME_INVALID`, ask for the canonical name explicitly rather than assuming the suggestion.\n4. `--into <dir>` places the package outside `packages/`; `--json` gives you the plan, its per-file hashes and a `fingerprint`. Exit 0 planned or applied, exit 1 refused — so **read `modeReason`, not the exit code**, to know whether anything was written; an explicit `--dry-run` beats `--apply` and still exits 0.\n5. A plan reserves nothing. `--apply` re-checks the target and answers `TARGET_CLAIMED` if another process got there first. An interrupted earlier run blocks nothing: its staging is reported as `staleStaging` for you to remove, never deleted automatically and never treated as a lock.\n6. The scaffold cannot tell you the identity is unique in the application — it checks the target directory only. Compose the package, then run `crm app inspect --json`.\n\n## Author it against the public contract\n\n1. Declare identity: `packageContract: 1`, a canonical `name` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`), a positive integer `version`, a bounded `label` and `description`.\n2. Declare `resources` — the record modules you own. Two packages claiming one resource is a startup failure, and declaring them is what makes that detectable.\n3. Import **only** from `packages/core/index.js`. Anything under `packages/core/src/*` is private; `package validate` fails a package that reaches into it. If something you need is not exported, that is a missing public export or a missing runtime capability — raise it, do not deep-import.\n4. Records are ordinary manifests applied by the module factory. Evidence records are `\"writable\": \"managed\"` throughout, so they are `get`/`list` publicly and exist only through your actions.\n5. Actions are ordinary action definitions: same runtime, transaction, audit, events and trace. Versioned decisions go in policies with declared JSON-safe `config` so the ADR-015 fingerprint covers them; handlers stay deterministic, synchronous and total.\n6. `metadata()` returns plain data only — no function, credential or path. Include a `notModeled` list: the limits are part of the contract.\n\n## Cross-package dependencies\n\n1. Reach another package **only** through a declared capability: `requires: [{package, capability, version}]`, opened with `domains.capability({consumer, capability, version, context: {modules, actor, now}})`.\n2. Pass your own runtime handles as the context, so the capability participates in **your** transaction. That is what makes a cross-package commit atomic without sharing a database handle.\n3. Offer the smallest interface that does the job — methods, never a service, table or query handle. Version it; a new shape is a new version.\n4. Never deep-import another package's source. An undeclared reach is refused at runtime and a cycle is refused at startup, but the rule is the design, not the guardrail.\n\n## Register, validate, prove\n\n1. Registration is one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. No dynamic import, no `eval`, no remote install, no marketplace, no hot loading.\n2. Walk the three commands in order; they answer three different questions and none of them substitutes for another:\n\n```text\ncrm package scaffold <name>  an empty package that already conforms\ncrm package inspect <dir>    what does it declare, own, offer and need?\ncrm package validate <dir>   is that declaration structurally valid?\ncrm package test <dir>       does it hold up when a real application composes it?\nnpm test -- <your suite>     does it do the right thing?   ← only your tests answer this\nnpm run verify               does the whole project still hold?\n```\n\n3. `crm package test` (DX4) composes your package into a throwaway copy of the project and boots it twice — with it and without it. It proves the declaration, the boundaries, every composition refusal, module manifests and migration identity, attach and detach, and that `app inspect` describes the same package you declared. **Generic conformance is not domain correctness**: no action is executed, no policy is evaluated and no state transition is driven. Every such gap is a named limitation in the report, starting with `DOMAIN_CORRECTNESS_NOT_PROVEN`. Your business JTBDs still need package-specific tests.\n4. All three commands **import** your package, and `test` also boots it, so your code runs with the CLI's authority. The harness does not intentionally mutate the caller project, and `test` imports your package from a throwaway copy rather than from your tree — but process isolation protects the invoking process from crashes, global-state changes and hangs, and is **not** a filesystem, network or OS sandbox. Never describe it as one.\n5. **Do not patch the kernel to make a check pass.** A conformance failure is a statement about your package. If you are certain it is a statement about the framework instead, say so in the PR with the exact check id and evidence — and if it is a horizontal gap, it goes through the Compatibility Backfill Rule in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`, not through a local fix.\n6. If your action targets a record another **package** owns, declare a capability of that package in `requires`. Without it, `package test` fails you with `UNDECLARED_PACKAGE_RECORD_DEPENDENCY` and names what the owner offers — because your package cannot be composed into any project that lacks the owner, and nothing in your declaration says so. Records **no** package owns are different: they belong to the host application, every package here acts on `order`, and depending on them needs no declaration. If no capability of the owner expresses what you do, that is a real seam gap: say so in the PR rather than working around it.\n7. Prove optionality: the same project without your package boots and behaves identically, and removing the import leaves the data alone.\n8. Reuse the shared package-conformance helper where the project ships one (`tests/helpers/package-conformance.js` in this repository); otherwise write the same assertions yourself. Then add an end-to-end test that drives your action over the real HTTP/SDK path in a clean project.\n9. Ship a README: what it owns, what it needs, how to enable it, what it deliberately does not do.\n\n## Claims discipline\n\nWhere the project carries a jobs matrix (`docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` in this repository), update it conservatively — a row moves only with linked evidence, and a data model is not a completed job. Where it carries none, the rule still holds against whatever the project claims in its README and its package metadata. Never describe a stored reference as access, permission or an account.\n\n## Do not implement here\n\nA package registry, npm publication, remote install, auto-update, cryptographic signing, a marketplace, hot loading, or any kernel patch. The scaffold (DX3) exists and stops at an identity on purpose — do not extend it to generate domain semantics, to edit `packages/domains/generated/index.js`, or to run a migration. See `docs/plans/dx3-package-scaffold.md`.\n\nFinish with `npm run verify`, the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`) and `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md`; leave the PR open for adversarial review.\n\n## What the contract enforces, and what it does not (ADR-018 addendum 4)\n\n1. The registry's indexes are private. `domains.get(name)` returns a **frozen public summary** — never a definition, never another package's `create()` or policy handlers. Do not look for a second route to an interface: there isn't one, and adding one is a kernel change.\n2. A capability opens only for a consumer that declared it, from the package the declaration named. Declare the requirement; do not reach around it.\n3. `metadata()` may add to your schema block but may **never restate** what the registry computes — `version`, `label`, `description`, `resources`, `requires`, `provides`, `actions`, `policies`. Those keys are refused at startup. It must return plain, function-free, JSON-safe data.\n4. Package `name` is bounded (64 characters), lowercase-canonical, and Map-keyed.\n5. Nothing here is a sandbox. `crm package validate` **imports and executes** your `src/index.js`; your actions and policies run in-process with full authority; and the consumer name passed when opening a capability is asserted by the caller. Never describe any of this as isolation.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: Create a partner-management domain package for this Accordo repository. Scope: manage a directory of external partner organizations as a standalone bounded domain — partner records (name, tier/status, primary contact info), lifecycle actions (e.g. onboard, activate, suspend), invariants and evidence, exposed via API/Admin per the standard package pattern. This is distinct from the existing lightweight `delivery-partner-engagement` module, which is only a name snapshot reference on a delivery project and must not be duplicated or broken. Follow docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md, register the package in packages/domains/generated/index.js, and finish with an ExecPlan and npm run verify per AGENTS.md."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"4e81f78b-9372-4890-8c32-4709126bebde","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:57:37.476Z","isSynthetic":true}
